[[Mughle]] [[İzmir]] 3) Ahmet Kenan Tanrıkulu [[İzmir]] 4) Edip Semih Yalçın [[İstanbul]] [[Mersin]] 6) Emin Haluk Ayhan [[Denizli]] [[İstanbul]] [[Ankara]] 9) Ahmet Selim Yurdakul [[Antalya]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Aydın]] [[Ankara]] [[Kayseri]] [[Samsun]] [[& & & & &gt; &gt; ……]] [[Adana]] [[Afyonkarahisar]] [[Adana]] [[Osmaniye]] [[Scholarship]] [[Erzurum]] Although our country has an ecologically appropriate environment for beekeeping, unfortunately, it uses very little of its potential. In view of the potential used, unfortunately, the amount of honey produced per hive is below average. In countries such as Canada and China, the product per hive weighed over 50 kilograms, but in our country, unfortunately, it weighed 15.67 kilograms. The number of bees in the world is quite low, considering the number of bees in the world is number two. As of honey production, our country ranked fourth in the world's production of honey is 5.7 per cent. Unfortunately, it is also observed that our country has no potential to produce bee products, such as bees' milk, pollen propus, bee venom, and cosmetics. According to recent statistics in Turkey, 54,574 families are beekeepers with 5m785,371 hives. The total production of 74.5 tonnes of honey yields $3372 million to our country's economy. In our country, all the beekeepers are traveling beekeepers. In the process, our beekeepers face some bureaucratic barriers, which affect the beekeeping industry in a negative way. In the beekeeping industry, where the concept of time is very important, bureaucratic procedures need to be facilitated. For the future of beekeeping, it is important that legal arrangements be made immediately according to the actual conditions of beekeeping in our country. Since there are no proper inspections of beekeepers' materials, problems in bee health require production and implementation of bee health projects across the country. In our cities with the potential for beekeeping, experts who can inspire and instruct our manufacturers to implement the right methods need to be trained and employed. It also reduces the efficiency of these technical staffs, which are already inadequate to deal with different issues. Illegal access to our country must be blocked by honey, wax and other bee products. Artificial honeys that the bee does not produce should not be allowed to market. Otherwise, it is a danger to the health of our citizens, and it is also a victim to our fellow beekeepers. Today, it is the centre of pine honey, which is first in the country with its hives and honey production; in the village of 334, where there are 5,800 families who are beekeepers in a total of 1,800 families, offering sweeteners as honey in tourist facilities. In this context, our Grand Assembly has great duties to investigate the measures that must be taken, including identifying the current problems of our beekeepers, resolving these problems, and the legal arrangements to be made. In order for our Supreme Assembly to carry out this mission, we respectfully request and demand the opening of parliamentary research under Article 104 and 105 of the Constitution's 98th and TBM Council. While many countries around the world have learned from recent nuclear accidents, the decision to shut down nuclear plants has been launched by AKP governments to build the Akkuyu Nuclear Power Plant in Mersin. One of the most important examples of devastating, destructive consequences of nuclear accidents is the Chernobyl accident. Despite the loss of thousands of lives and many more of them have been crippled, problems continue after 25 years. In the Chernobyl region, it has been found that children have increased thyroid cancer by 200 percent, there are significant increases in other cancer rates, birth rates are rising, people's genetic structures are degraded. Furthermore, the land that was contaminated by this accident will not be farmed for hundreds of years. Those who claim that important lessons have been learned from the Chernobyl accident and have made great progress in preventing nuclear accidents have decided to shut down nuclear plants after the recent Fukushima accident. Even when there were no accidents in nuclear plants, the radiation surge around the plant is known to have negative effects on life as a result of air, water, soil pollution. Scientific research has shown that the risk of blood cancer has increased 2.2 times higher in children living within five kilometers of the plant without any accidents or business mistakes in a nuclear plant. Another problem for nuclear plants is that there is no known way to destroy and destroy nuclear waste, and there is no worldwide method for safe disposal of these wastes. The cooling water is also another problem when nuclear power plants that are given to the sea destabilise the marine ecosystem. It increases sea water temperature by 2-6 centigrats, causing the species of the sea to become extinct and the radiation that spreads to the sea into the food chain through fish. As a result, no energy is more valuable than human health. The nuclear power plant, which was planned to be established in Akkuyu, is inevitable to establish a research commission in order to avoid further harming the region, human health, environment, and the future of the state of the Republic of Turkey.
